data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:12 | [2024-06-28 13:59] derbymanager : - I'm tweaking school training to be faster, hopefully it'll be better in the next days. Main squad training is working well. - Mentors have been simplified, they now give a fixed boost (the number written next to them)  to all senior and youth players in their group. The number is not divided by the number of players anymore. Each 1 is equal to 0.05 facility stars (e.g. 10 = half a star). Maximum is 30 points in a group (1.5 stars). - Slowed down form change for players. Previously they could jump almost a full step in a single day (e.g. they could go from superb to fine in only 2 days). - Board satisfaction is calculated daily based on your league position. It moves slower if you are new to the team and/or it's the beginning of the season. The baseline is 75% (you are exactly at season expectation) and better or worse position affects the baseline. For example, your expectation is to be 6th but you are 12th for the first half of the season, satisfaction will move down every day in that period. Then you get 3 wins in a row and get to 6th, it will go up a bit towards 75% but it won't be enough to completely negate the first half of the season. But if you continue to win and end up 1st at the end, by that time satisfaction should go above 75% (even though you were below the expectation in the first half of the season, the second half matters more and will probably put you somewhere above 75% in the end).
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:15 | [2024-07-16 12:51] derbymanager : - Scouting players doesn't improve their chance to sign for you, it only reveals details about them. - 3rd scout will reduce the time needed for the player to reach his decision to 6-12 hours (only if the decision was further away than that). - Players won't sign new contracts if they're 37 or older (so at 36 is the last train to extend them to 39). - When a player's contract expires he's gonna stay in the club on a daily rolling contract. Such players:  - Still cost salary and take up salary space according to their old contract,  - Are transfer listed (from the day their contract expired, not two weeks before that),  - Are super unhappy (remember, this only matters for team spirit if you play them, but they're less likely to extend contract unhappy),  - Have 0% training effort no matter if they play or not, meaning they can't train and are more likely to drop skills,  - Their form can only go down or stagnate, it can't go up (this is now the case also for free agents),  - Their price will drop over time up to 10-15% (this is also the case for free agents).
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:43 | [2024-10-28 09:43] derbymanager : I've split tactical options to better follow the engine and to allow for certain combinations that were not possible before:  - Build up type - specifies in which area of the pitch will your team try to build up their attacks. Selecting the Counters option means your team will start counters more often or, when that isn’t possible, will use the mixed approach. - Build up style - is used to shift some of the power from one build up type towards the other. Careful will move power from creativity to pressing but only if not losing, risky will do the opposite if not winning, and pressing will do it for counters only. - Passing type - specifies what kind of passes will your players prefer from long passes that favor sending the ball as far ahead as possible to short passes that do the opposite. - Passing focus - tells your players to concentrate passing towards a certain area after the action-resolution part has started (as opposed to build up type that relates to the abstract build up part) - Threat focus - sets the preferred type of shots you’d like to create, if possible.  Your teams have been set to mixed for all of those so please update.  ***Other changes***  - Experience in the scout info panel is explained better with clear indication if a player has certain aspect of experience significantly higher or lower than the others.  - Spider graph now makes no sense, I have to think what to show there now. Any suggestions? - Training is now divided into 3 categories (school is gone) and it had to be reset for all teams so please update your teams. - Since training for specific event is now too easy, training gets a penalty the higher the skill (e.g. it's easier to bump a skill from 1 to 2 than it is from 19 to 20). - Some other changes that I can't even remember anymore. Report if you find some of the things broken etc...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:55 | [2025-01-08 21:34] derbymanager : - Added few more tier descriptions for weaker players. Check it out in wiki. Those tiers are shorter than the top ones (as indicated by the range shown in the wiki). This doesn't do anything except to give a bit more info about low tier players. - Scout will now tell you how likely the player is to reach a tier one or more steps ahead of him. How far ahead the scout looks is predetermined for each player and depends on age (scout generally looks farther ahead for younger players), for some you'll see the next step but with greater accuracy, for others you'll see farther ahead but with less precision. Scout tries to take into account player's age and some other factors but it isn't guarantee of anything as growth depends on club, playtime, luck in addition to potential that the scout looks at.  *E.g. for two fringe players (lowest tier) you could see: *high potential to reach lower league tier* and *low potential to become a solid player*. Their potential could be the same but the wording is different. The first one tells you that he can grow, maybe even beyond the solid player, maybe not. The second one at least guarantees he has enough potential to reach solid but unlikely to go all the way to the world class (although possible).*

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:113 | [2026-06-17 06:06] derbymanager : New update dropped yesterday late evening, market now has regions to explore, school is different, and it works like this:  **School and youngsters** - Each of the big 5 countries/regions has 400 youngsters at any time, other regions have 250 youngsters. - Every start of the season the system releases all players from schools, puts some players that are too old or too weak on the free market, repopulates the pool back to 400, and redistributes players in some logic so that top teams have a bit higher chance to get better youngsters. - You can't re-roll players anymore, you get what you get and you can only promote someone to the first team to get a new player, most likely a bad one. - Since the pool is bigger than what the teams need for schools, the remaining players are left on the free transfer and can be discovered if you scout that region. - They all grow the same, the difference is that you can sign your school players for free while those free youngsters act like any other free player and will want a signing fee and real salary. They also need to be discovered and scouted so there is luck involved.  **Regions**
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:115 | [2026-06-17 09:21] derbymanager : - When you scout a player (not the send scout action but scouting individual players) there is a chance that that player will leak to other managers.   - Each region has some default chance for leaks. The chance is also higher for better players and multiplied with each manager's knowledge (as explained above). So if you have 0 knowledge of a region, there is 0 chance to catch a leak.   - When you do catch a leak it also improves your knowledge of that region (it counts as a discovered player) and thus improves your chances for future leaks.   - Leaks are not especially announced but all discovered players show up in the list tab so if a good player is leaked to you and you are searching for players you will see him.  *So, having a basic knowledge of all regions will show you most of the best players that you can buy, having high knowledge in fewer regions will auto-discover players for you. Scouting a region where there are many managers will leak your players, scouting obscure region reduces the chances of a leak and may keep you as the only bidder on a good player.*  You all got +20 scouts to play around with regions. The system will refresh them to 20 tonight so spend them asap.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:116 | [2026-06-18 07:35] derbymanager : There is some confusion so I'll write it here:  - You can see all club-based players on the market that are listed from the 5 playable leagues. These are usually also the best players in the world since majority of top teams (and thus top players) are from these 5 leagues. - You can scout for more players, mostly youngsters, from 11 regions:   - The 5 playable regions will only have youngsters there since players with a team are auto-visible in the list tab already. So only youngsters are left.   - The remaining 6 regions have a mix of youngsters and club-based players. When you scout those the chance is you'll discover club-based players first as the system leans towards discovering better players first and club-based players are usually better. Players you can't buy (that are too good for your team) aren't discoverable so you're always getting the best players that you can buy. - The number of discovered players in a region increases the chance you'll auto-discover more players in that region as someone else scouts them. - To be able to bid on a player you need to scout him individually at least once to discover his price and demands.  That's all there is to it.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:1304 | [2023-05-25 13:39] derbymanager : Both are in game, the logic behind who knows is a bit different.  - Current way, news travel through your country and your direct competitors can find out about your target. Someone might check that player and decide to go for him. There is real risk involved in going public because not-interested people might get interested. But also, you might miss interested people. Even from your country, a manager might simply miss the news.  - Your way, there are no news (I can't target individual managers) but whoever spends 2 scouts sees it. It's safe to assume, since you have nothing to lose, that everyone interested will spend two scouts before buying. So everyone interested will see it. The only difference is that you won't gather extra interest (which is the fun part).  When you know that there is some interest your best approach is to go with 3rd scout as well. Not that you can't catch the player with 2 scouts only, but your chances will be better with 3 scouts. There is a bit of decision making if there is only one interested team, you might want to sneak up on that team. But in that case:  - Current way, you can't be sure if there is more than one interested team. You have to assume there are more. But the first team, the one already interested, can try to sneak in a low offer because: a) maybe nobody joins the bidding war, and b) even if they see the news they might think it's already too late as maybe more than one team is bidding... It's harder to make decision if you don't have complete information.  - Your way, you know exactly that there is only one interested team and that you have to offer more. Since that interested team can also check if someone else joined knows that you can 100% see that they're interested - they shouldn't make a weak offer. So this approach falls apart. It actually puts the team with 3rd scout at a very vulnerable position and benefits everyone else... So, don't use 3rd scout.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:4690 | [2023-07-13 15:06] .jarles : Here is my take on the scout/player skill debate... <@530694771900153871>   I highly prefer a transparent system, where we can see and do the following:  __All skills are displayed in ranges:__ 1-5 (Exact number provided after scout report) 6-10 (Exact number provided after scout report) 11-15 (Exact number provided after scout report) 16-20 (Exact number provided after scout report) Work rate: Bad-Superb (Hidden until 1 scout report completed) Determination: Bad-Superb (Hidden until 1 scout report completed) Focus: Bad-Superb (Hidden until 1 scout report completed)  I would argue saying that there is a very big difference for a defender to have 11 or 15 in the important defensive skills, just as there is a big difference in having 16 or 20 in the offensive skills for a forward. (Having 11 in ALL skills or 15 in all skills is a HUGE difference) The valuation of such a player would be very different, and a scout report is most often needed before making a bid.  Yes, the ranges helps "filtering" out the bad players - But that's relatively easy to do as well with the current system. Why make it tedious to find the players you need for your team?
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:4691 | [2023-07-13 15:06] .jarles : _ _ __What scouts do:__  1 report:  - Reveal "WDF" + Exact player skills. - Minor interest boost from the player - Scout evaluation (XP + Star rating)  2 reports:  - Moderate interest bonus from the player - Potential for the players best training category is revealed  3 reports: - Significant interest bonus from the player - Global news report about your interest in the player   ... The issue with opening many tabs can be fixed by adding a pop-up window when you hover over a players name. (Pop-up only displays player skills) (This could work both on the market page and squad page, to make it easier/faster to manage both aspects)  __Here is how the market page could look like:__  The "average" defensive/midfielder/offensive skill number works in the following way:  The skills below for the individual 3 categories are added together, and the average number is used to show the range. Example: For "MID" the passing is 13, the BC is 15 and the TQ is 11. This means the average is = 11. The number 11 is displayed in the range 11-15 (Like player skill)  Skills used to generate AVG number:  DEF:  Marking Tackling Awareness Positioning  MID: Passing Ball control Technique  OFF: Off the ball Finishing Accuracy  ... This way managers don't have to open up a lot of tabs, and the market becomes transparent, while still keeping a relative big focus/emphasis on scout reports.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:4963 | [2019-11-27 12:05] derbymanager : Debt alone isn't much of a problem, the issue was that there is an option to sell down to 20 players and buy 13 great ones (and I mean out of your league ones) that kick start you towards winning the league (or at least being close to it) which in turn brings in a ton of money from both wins and player growth. There is no real penalty for having small squads (a couple of games per season perhaps where you have many injuries/cards), which combined with (back then) huge prices for youth players (Gio had like 100M in youths at one point) allowed this to happen. Buy the best youngest player you can get your hands on and not only is he already better than most of the league - he will still grow and in 5 seasons you can sell him for huge profit, right before his price predictably begins to drop... data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5322 | [2019-12-02 09:24] derbymanager : Exactly. And I want top teams to release their 10M youth players as those players would be key players for me... The idea is that when you do find your Messi or whoever that you want to keep him as long as possible, even into his 30s. Replacing him is a project that may or may not end well. Since club fit is always averaged to 50% it means that out of 20 players in linear distribution 2 will have 95% fit, 2 will have 85%... and 2 will have 5% fit. It won't be that precise ofc, maybe you'll have 10 players at 45% and 10 at 55%... Probably a bit of both. So when you find your 95% players you have to consider how could you replace him and keep the same level of performance before you sell him. This also means that you can sell your B team and play with 16 players and fill in with cheapest bastards you can - but statistically, some of your cheapest bastards will have high club fit and some of your first 16 will have bad... What (I hope) you will want to have is 25-30 players that cover a wide range of skills, some b team materials, some top notch players, and a lot in between...

TQ will contribute to long shots more and so will IF, WB will contribute to crosses while FB won't etc... I'll give exact numbers once I fix everything around here, in the meantime stumble around and experiment :) As for versatility - it's just a hidden stat that allows player to play a bit better when out of position. Such players will also change their role easily compared to players without versatility. Finally, **effort** is current effort the player puts in training. It depends on club/role fit but it is random. It will change every couple of days so your player might have 14%, 87%, 74%... I will add a graph one day, for now it's just how much effort he puts into training.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5857 | [2019-12-10 09:58] saltylem0n_ : 3) Team dynamics  - The 28 player rule, especially in its latest iteration servers no purpose in the game. - All players will obviously build a squad as big as they want to challenge the league. - If someone can do this with 15 players as in season 13 - good for you. - If someone wants to have 30 players - go ahead. - Asking players to stay between 28-32 players just so they avoid some 20% team spirit penalty is not a good reason. - Training at top clubs is ruined. - The whole fantasy of buying wonderkids in top clubs and training them to become superstars is nonexistent. - Buying a top talent in a good club means he needs to play to get some training. - That leads to forcing managers to rotate smaller squads to be more training efficient. - Youth players / B team players have been reduced to a passive income commodity at top clubs, because they stop training at 2.5 stars. - 2.5 stars is so far away from 1st team quality that there is seriously nothing else you can do with those players but sell them. - Youth players at lower tier clubs are also useless, because those clubs will buy the A tier club youths on the market and play them. - I like the effort %, but I think it should be tied to something and be more like a constant instead of a variable. - There are 0 indicators for player/club fit - this can be the one. - However you cannot have a constant effort % if training is a variable and sometimes even 0. - What happened to all the bot B teams and why are they full of old players? Did you change something? Where did all the young players go?  -----------
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5859 | [2019-12-10 09:59] saltylem0n_ : 5) What I would like to see in the game  - More control, freedom and flexibility with finances. Generating more money should not be reduced to employing a slightly better staff member. - Bring back the excitement back into training. Too many restrictions right now. - Roles are doing the exact opposite of what they were meant to do in the way they are implemented right now. Allowing managers to pick the role a player has on the field will allow that flexibility. - The B team has no purpose right now. I would have kept them as youth teams where each team were supposed to have a 11 players that form a proper lineup, not just 7 GK and 3 DR. Youth teams had a charm, and were a feature that could have been expanded. As of right now the B team (especially for bots) is an extension of the first team with a different training regime. - Youth intake (one of the best feature in FM) is a complete miss in DM. No one knows when it happens, no one knows what to expect and when you get a youth player, the feeling is almost always anticlimactic. Motorsport Manager 3 has a fantastic youth program feature that gets you involved with your youths way before you get to know what their potential is and allows you to influence them before their final ability is revealed to you. - Training should be changed in a way that allows the player to influence the players, while at the same time keeping the existing random stats improvement that differentiate players between one another.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5881 | [2019-12-11 00:42] derbymanager : The reason I introduced penalties for B team and first team training is to give incentive to use youth players. If you can get your B team players to grow to 4 stars before you start playing them - that doesn't really pose as a risk. And you can't just grow him to 4 stars and sell him like a commodity for millions of money.  My idea is that you can introduce a couple of B players to your main squad slowly and over time. Basically, you would get a 1 star player, after some time he would grow to 2 stars so you would start playing him occasionally, then 3 stars and you would treat him as rotation player, then 4 stars and key status etc... Unless of course he doesn't fit with your club/plans and he gets sold to some other club where he can continue his progress.  The reason for those steps is to help bots deal with their squads. Those that play will get training which will help keep bots competitive as their first team will have 3-5 stars and the rest will have fewer. Which is how things work in real life. There is a strong correlation between first team players and prices 🙂 Simply put, those that play get seen and gather interest...  I don't see how this makes you sell youth players too early at top clubs or why there isn't a point to having youth players in C. Maybe if you consider B team solely as money generator it is now a bit less profitable but that's not the intention of B teams. And you can still make it work, you only need to buy 5M players instead of 10M for your youth/B squad, grow them to 2 stars and sell for profit.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5942 | [2019-12-12 15:21] derbymanager : To quickly go through latest comments: -  On penalties fiasco: All penalties are presented, there are no hidden penalties if you use players as expected. I don't want to explicitly state that there is a penalty and how much if you play a GK as AMC... Same as box to box midfielders - if you play them as box to box - the penalty is 0% or up to 10% if not their preferred position (which is shown on the info screen)... Calling it a fiasco is a bit of overreaction imo :) - On playing youths: The old training system had little to do with peak and a lot to do with every player growing no matter what. There were no failed youths as they couldn't fail. Also, it is only expensive if you have 2-star players in B. It doesn't really cost a lot of money if you have 1 star youths, the salaries are affected by sharpness so it will cost you less than before (at least relatively speaking, compared to first team players whose salaries have grown with the last update). Since price isn't so dependant on skills anymore it will now be easier (once I balance everything) to actually have Ansu Fatis in your team, but also to have Bojans... - On player's role/club fit: There will be indicators how fans perceive your players that will give you a good idea who is where. The more consistently they play - the more precise explanation you'll get. If a player has maximum club fit but you play him 10 games in 5 seasons you might get info that he shows promise... If he is a regular starter for seasons - you will get much more defined explanation of where he stands. In addition to that you'll have training effort that will indicate how well he fits with the club and average ratings to compare how well he plays. There won't be exact number because that would turn the game into chasing that one number, but a number of indicators will be shown and it will be up to you to decide...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:9826 | [2020-05-06 18:33] derbymanager : As for suggestions about stuff, there's some good stuff there, a lot of it I've already tried, some things just require a lot of work for a little effect etc... I'll just quickly give a reasoning what I'll probably do. It is not final but after reading all of it, this is what I think.  Giving free BM is in essence the same as giving more money to every team, which won't work due to how the game balances itself. Also, picking sponsors has a mobile game feel to it and I'm strongly against mechanics that require you to be online at specific time. Preventing injuries, getting better youths and anything that depends on random will fail. I talked about that some time ago. This could be done by taking control over randomness so that every team gets the same. But it would be weird if you are guaranteed 80 days worth of injuries in a season... Also, this is not such game, teams and luck are just not the same for everyone.  Also, somehow it got accepted that BM is the ultimate best staff member to have and nobody else comes close to him, which I don't believe is true.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:9828 | [2020-05-06 18:47] derbymanager : S is a wild card, it gives you more of randomly generated value that is dependable on several parameters. You could get 0.5M player with huge potential, how much is that worth to you? If you train him - a lot. If you sell him right away - well, it's not worth it. You can also scout more so there is a better chance of finding the right player (who can generate money through wins or development), although this is of limited usability I'm aware.  So, the things I think about doing: - adding a penalty for having a lot of the same staff. This should release some of BM for others to use and will reduce their money generating power in bulk, while keeping it good on individual level. The penalty won't be so harsh as to prevent you from stacking, but it will try to discourage you from doing that. - making free training smaller so you'll need coaches to train players effectively as Kum suggested. Again, the change won't be huge, just a nudge in that direction. I will also increase the B team training to approx 3 stars so that you can train your youngsters a bit further before you have to promote them. - connecting the mentoring bonus from oldies with the staff so that having coaches and mentors is the optimal way to train players. Maybe youngsters will benefit from this more or something, I have to think about it still but I don't like it the way it is now...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:13344 | [2020-09-30 10:35] derbymanager : Hehe, thanks :D I talked with poletz a lot and my point was (is) that everything in DM is a bit obscured. Everything is shown but not exactly precise so that managers must decide what bears more weight. Except for those skills. So it's partially a matter of principle. I honestly didn't think anyone would "decipher" them 😄 I didn't.  But then I looked at it and noticed that some positions relied heavily on one or two skills, while others needed like 5 to be functional. When I created it my goal was to make every skill more or less equally useful overall (with no regard if they dominate in certain areas), but the goal should be to make each area balanced on its own (as is now). A lot has changed since I first created that part so maybe it made sense 10 seasons ago. But today - this could be meta gamed (not saying that it was) and it kind of ruined the experience for me. I, too, want to train and buy players that will get me the best results. And if that part of the game is solved - then there is no decision making.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:514 | [2023-02-22 22:18] derbymanager : Anyway, a short update is that I have: - The team selection and views are done but barebones - The match engine still needs some work to be shitty but usable - Calendar needs changing, I've decided 1 season will last 2 months - Training system is in place for the most part - The basics of a scouting system are in place - Currently working on contracts and transfers - Support for user accounts and other "infrastructural" things is mostly done When I polish all of that to the point that it's usable (I know, not really a definition of the word polish) I will release the game.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:540 | [2023-02-23 13:44] derbymanager : I decided because of a couple of reasons: - I always felt like seasons went by too fast in old DM. For me at least. Now two months is maybe a bit too long for my taste but I wanted to try it. I was thinking 1.5 months but that would make it complicated to track... - We will have transfer windows this time so there needs to be a couple of days free in the "winter" to make transfers. - I want to make the world more vivid. With only 6 leagues I want to put a spotlight on each of them. So each league will get its own hour and the results will be among the news etc... Especially the international competitions... With 2 games per day it would feel cluttered I think. - Some people had trouble setting up their squads because their game was during the night or they were unavailable for those 6 hours between the first and second game... Now it'll be 1 game per day only and cca 24 hours to update. - I think there will be youth games in later versions so that'll be the 2nd game of the day to check out.  I think that's it.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:556 | [2023-02-24 11:51] derbymanager : I can set youth games to happen on off-days so that there's always something going on for you.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:563 | [2023-02-26 10:27] derbymanager : Yeah, transfer windows could be interesting as your knowledge will be quite limited. So, basically, you'll have 3 scouts per day and you can scout a player 3 times. First scout gives you insight on skills and basic info, second reveals a bit more but also establishes contact, giving you better terms in contract negotiations, and finally 3rd scout is publicly stating interest, at which point the news show up in everyone's timeline that you're interested. This 3rd scout further improves your chances at contract negotiations and can also make a player unsettled in his team if there are right conditions (i.e. you are the bigger club, he is already slightly unhappy etc.), although the chance of that happening is low. But yeah, you can screw around with other teams and try to fuck them up 🙂
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:567 | [2023-02-26 12:34] derbymanager : Yes. There will be an indicator - i.e. this player is world class or a promising youngster. Just a general category. Also his stats. Then as you scout you get more info.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:592 | [2023-03-22 12:52] derbymanager : So, I can't decide on season length and I have to make that choice soon because other things depend on it...  Option 1: Season lasts 2 months, 1 game per day with some days having no games if you're out of cups. Periods after 28th in the month are without games. Option 2: Season lasts 6 weeks. There is a league game every day and cup games are approx. every second day in later times. Option 3: Season lasts 1 month. There are 2 games almost every day.  I was thinking of going with option 1 but when I created players' contracts and they lasted for 6 months it seemed too far in the future. I now cut contracts to 2 seasons only (4 months) but still it feels quite slow. Especially since there will be transfer windows, so you might sign a player beginning of March, he would join in April, and his contract would last until September... The good side of option 1 was to allow for youth teams and games in the evening hours but I'm uncertain if this is something that would be interesting to most people. They're basically just friendlies.  As I already said, option 3 with 1 month season seemed too short for me. It was squeezed tight and I think some people felt under pressure to sign in twice per day just to play. On the other hand, it created some nice history. We actually did manage to see 2-3 generations of players emerge from this schedule and there was always something happening... However, going this route would definitely mean cutting out youth matches, but I was thinking of implementing some kind of loaning system for youngsters perhaps. In this case I think there would be only one transfer window between seasons (no winter window). Definitely no youth teams as we had previously. Maybe B teams the way Barcelona and Real have them (teams that couldn't progress to 1st division)...  Finally, I was playing around with 6 weeks seasons (option 2). Two windows but no youth teams. The biggest problem here is not knowing when the next season starts (it's not 1st in the month).

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:837 | [2023-03-23 10:00] derbymanager : Yeah, I didn't count the "votes" but it seems ???? is the outlier that wants 1 month season. Most people are for 6 weeks or 2 months.  6 weeks: - A schedule is a bit tight. Basically only 4 days without games and those who do well in the cups have 3 games in 2 days on average. I could make the league have 18 teams, that would remove 4 games and would give more free days. But in that case we would still need few days where there are two games and I don't like it... - Tempo is still pretty fast and we can go through almost 9 seasons in 1 year.  2 months: - I schedule is a bit loose 🙂 For some teams there might be up to 20 free days in those 2 months. - Tempo seems too slow. Some really well optimised teams could have a strong skeleton of 5-6 players that could let them dominate for a full real time year without the need to change much. Don't know if it'll happen, but waiting is the opposite of fun for me. Waiting for someone to start losing or waiting for scout reports to come in, doesn't matter 🙂 - The benefit is that there is always only 1 game per day (or 0) and I can track injuries in days (with other calendars I can't, it has to be in games, which isn't a huge deal) and I can use the 1st of the month for season start (also not a huge deal tbh).  Overall, I don't see much benefit of going with 2 months and I can't really estimate how big of a deal are the cons of 2 month calendar as it's quite subjective for each of us...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:1279 | [2023-05-02 08:33] derbymanager : I feel TS was great for doing what it had to do: prevent massive revamps of the team in two weeks. People used to sell 15 players and buy 15 back without losing their spot at the top. It made top teams untouchable. The best approach was to sell 2 good players and 3 average ones to buy 3 great players and 2 shitty ones.  With TS you had to spread that over 3 seasons making the whole thing a bit more realistic. The real underlying problem was that youths had almost guaranteed price growth, but it delivered elegantly a solution that didn't require a rewrite of half of the game...

The problem with training groups is that those skills are not equally useful for each position. Extreme example but handling was under technical skills. Since I have to work with a lot of player variables and combinations of positions and training types it was basically impossible for me to make it fair... data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:12823 | [2025-01-02 10:23] skuld6666 : Welcome 👍   OK here is my quick 'day 1' Derby Manager guide.....  1) You can play DM quite intuitively in a lot of ways. The usual formations work well such as 4-4-2, 4-5-1 etc. Base this on your best players available at the moment. Your first quest is to win your next game or not get beat. This is nearly always the next most important thing 🙂  2) So initially go carefully through your team and find your best 11 as a starting point. The star ratings really help there to see who is good at your current level.  3) Then you can further filter by looking at their experience level (more is always good). Then their natural fitness (if it's bad they may be a liability longer term), look at the current form, potential etc. Bad (red) form you can consider perhaps bringing in someone else for that game if there is another OK option etc.  4) A blend of younger and older players is often a good starting point - older/experienced is good for stable performances and younger for growth and to help you in future. Like in real life do you really want a talented, but unreliable 18 year old in goal?   5) Final thing for now. Look at depth in each position. You need at least 2 players that can cover each position. Your youth school can help you out in an emergency here (say you have no backup GK etc). Probably about 21-22 players is a good squad number.  6) Then look at the next opposition. Do they score a lot? Concede a lot? Or no? Do they have key players out? What formation do they generally play? Do they have an obvious star to try and stop?  Look at all these things and adjust your tactics to be a bit more defensive/attacking depending on how ambitious you think you can be and their strengths and weaknesses.  7) Then look at the players left over. How is their potential? Do they need a few games to help them train? Can you sell some who are useless and not improving? Or retire some to remove their wasted wage?

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:17089 | [2026-01-27 03:24] furysk : This game is about doing 3 things right:  Managing your Team Managing your Money Managing your Facilities  To manage your team, you must have young and old players, or the young play poorly.  You must manage their condition or they get hurt.  You must also be aware of the semi-hidden talents work which are described in the <#1102892256630943754> under skills, and know what being a team player versus individualistic does to how they commit to actions.  To manage your money, you must make sure to take the care in evaluating players coming into your team rather than doing so randomly.  You must understand how the finances page works, and understand how to generate more money at each level so you can balance you cash and also your facilities  to manage your facilities, you must understand the implication of training based facilities on your prospects, and sort out which facility has the most advantage to upgrade when and why.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:18558 | [2026-09-28 00:53] furysk : For generic advice now that sports are done for the day over here in the USA, i'd say that the following would apply to this game:  1) Finances are a lot of what makes or breaks your ability in tier 4.  The more wage budget and cash you can generate, the more margin for error or corrections you have.  the stadium in tier 4 capacity wise is .5 * stadium size * 10.  So if your stadium is 23000, the maximum you can make is 115,000.  Your team's reputation is largely responsible for the stadium filling out, and your team is at about 65k in estimated stadium revenue.  So you wont get any cash from upgrading the stadium any time soon, and would honestly need a 200's reputation to even push into the 80k-90k range.  any stadium upgrade is just losing you the cash spent to get the upgrade + the increase in Maintenance.  Sponsors on the other hand give you an additional 50k outright wage budget by adding 50k to the sponsorship deals, and if you spend an additional 30k cash you still have 20k in effective cash flow gained and 50k in wage budget gained still.  2) I would suggest while newer to place 5 players in defensive positions and 5 players in offensive positions.  Where on the pitch they are means not as much to me, just making sure enough go back to defend in a play is important, and enough go forward and generate chances equally important.  If you run a 3-4-3, in theory you should have 2 defensive midfielders, 3 defensive backs, and then two distribution/forward-thinking midfielders and a TQ/FN and a TM/CF up front (or if you want to use wings, then maybe IF's, or WF's).  As you learn more you can get away from these sorts of even measurements and experiment more, but going all turtle defense or all offense are equally problematic depending.  3) Do not go all youth, they need experienced players help to grow in tier 4.  get a solid mix of experienced and youngsters, and work to keep players rotated so they can gain experience or not lose too much conditioning.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:22644 | [2024-05-28 20:44] yasen_gg : If you have an icon - this player can be a mentor If icon is blue (ON - you can click it) - player trains slightly worse, but provides bonus to every other player (including those in the Youth School) If the icon is white (OFF) - you've decided for this player to focus on his own training and not mentor.  A thing to note - the mentorship bonus depends on this players' effort. This means that you need to make sure this player plays regularly so that he can boost the training of others more efficiently.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:22645 | [2024-05-28 20:58] derbymanager : Also, mentor improves training only for players in the same group (gks/defenders/mids/forwards) across both senior and youth team.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:2713 | [2025-07-31 10:08] derbymanager : I realized I didn't answer almost any of your questions 😅   > Like what to do at the start - which players do I sell, which players are worth buying at the start? Try to have 15 players with 3-4 stars in your squad. Better to have a good average squad than few top players and the rest all shitty ones. > How does the yout school work, when to promote youths? For a season you can observe 4 players in youth school. After 3 weeks you'll see their potential and all skills. If you like them, you can hire them. If you don't, you can find a new player at any time. When they reach their training limit in the school there will be a message so you can promote them if you think they'll get some playtime in your squad.  > Are players who are reaching their peak at the club worth keeping or should I get rid of them? On the training screen you can see a yellow or red icon that the player is close to reaching their peak in your team. That means that they could still grow but would need a better team to do so. If you're about to get promoted it might be worth keeping them. Otherwise you might consider selling them and buying some that can grow or that won't be unhappy. Having a couple unhappy players is ok, having half of the team unhappy is problematic. There is no right or wrong way, assess your squad and decide. * > What is sharpness and how important is it? Sharpness is increased by playing games and it's pretty important but not so much to avoid playing a good player with low sharpness. It grows quickly and it's basically here to promote rotating your squad so that you'd have more than 11 players ready to play at all times. > How much do mentors really contribute to the training? Quite a lot. They boost your facilities. > If I got w low-division club am I stuck with it for a longer time and should prepare to build it or do I get offers from better teams relatively soon? You'll get offers from better teams when you go up into 3rd division and survive there.

AWB and DWG are too similar imo and will often take up the same space, so I'd use one or the other. HM and DLP together, especially with short passing, won't be able to get the ball forward fast enough to make use of the loan striker, especially when he's also not getting any crosses.   So to sum up, don't be scared to be more aggressive, tactics and role wise, get someone solid up front to score goals, tighten up the defense if possible with another signing, and most importantly, be patient, the game takes time, especially at a new team, but you have nothing to lose, so, Experiment. If you need any specific advise regarding tactics or roles just shout.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:3874 | [2026-03-03 18:14] furysk : The math as you’ll find is a bit complicated for keeping teams financially afloat without having to field full prospects so here is the gist of it.  The maintenance costs are I believe 30k at local, 40k at regional for each upgrade.  Sponsorship gives you 75k per facility to your total budget and your wage budgets. Stadium gives you more budget room before cutting into your upkeep, but only if you are maxing your stadium out (to find that number for you, [(seats times 10) times .5]. Or if your stadium shows 90000 in your specific 18k stadium). So that’s why sponsors for you gives you immediate relief and stadium will become important to upgrade as you climb but isn’t currently.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4917 | [2026-08-14 18:46] derbymanager : Think of facilities as a bet in the context of youths.   Obviously, training is one of the best ways to earn money in the main squad. Buy a player, train him, sell for more.  With youngsters the price is basically 0, or close to it, even if players are worth several million. So:  - small facilities: you can get just about anyone, train them, promote them, train them more, sell them for small profit. - big facilities: you need great youngsters, train them, promote them, train them more, sell them for big profit.  So, facilities help you train them but also lock you out of shitty youngsters. You need to find those 3M+ on the market, get them to 7M to turn profit. With low facilities (which should be in low divisions) you can take a 1M player, get him to 3M and still earn money.   Facilities just increase the stakes you are playing with. In a casino you would be at the black jack table with $1000 chips, while d4 team would drop coins into a slot machine 🙂

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4957 | [2026-08-15 14:55] derbymanager : So, to give a bit of an overview:  When you promote a player their price is lower because they haven't played any games so there is no performance multiplier on price yet. So the price you see when you promote someone from school is lower than what that player is actually worth. But if you use that price and find the player with the same price and age on the market, just compare the costs. Include the cost of school, contracts, everything until the end of their first professional contract.  For someone with facilities in the 40s total cost for 1 school year + 3 first team years is about 6M. For someone with facilities in the 20s total cost is about 3M.  A 2M player will cost you about 5M to get from the market (with 3 years of salaries and the signing fee).  Financially, even if you miss and promote a bad player, you basically can't miss with youngsters. You would have to promote <1M players that can't grow in your squad and can't be sold to actually lose money... The fact is that the youngsters were and, especially now, are too good investment.   Which brings me to the real problem and that is the money drain from the game. Every youngster that gets promoted is money that goes out of the game. Keeping them in school drains the money from the game. Buying them from the free market into the school drains the money from the game. It affects the market negatively. D4 teams don't have to trade among themselves, they can get their entry-level players from schools instead. Why would you buy 2M player when you can get him for 0?  I like the system, I like scouting youngsters, but it's problematic (and not because you can't promote all of them). So expect even more updates with restrictions and discouragement from using them 😬

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part6.txt:3137 | [2026-06-20 17:12] furysk : My thoughts on the current system, and in general what i think is good or bad about it:  Good - It has brought the average age of a developing player down from what was basically 17-18 on the transfer list to 16-17.  That extra 30 days of development average for the people to utilize is very important imo in getting more mileage out of developing them, assuming their development age is close to their true age.    Good - the options available to train for lower level teams has expanded significantly.  Teams are less likely to settle on a few players in their school because of the age gap between school and the earliest available TL'd player (which takes a player exitting school or a team buying a guy out of school and TL'ing him at the earliest availability) and because of this more of the 'right guys' have a potential chance to be recruited in.  Bad - For higher level teams, this system is not nearly as helpful.  In some aspects, this design is a good thing, but they become reliant on finding a spot for lets say on average a 1M-1.5M at best prospect in their lineup card that would otherwise have a 10-12m player, meaning the cost of acquisition to them also has to include the potential value of losses incurred due to the fielding of that player, not just the amount they spent to bring them in.  Bad - it's a heavier overall investment cost now to find veteran players on the open market, because we're now dealing with not only lower understanding of veterans skills than when i first started but we are also dealing with a larger range of true valuation on the veteran players.  Bad - This system is reliant on human players not getting attached to a larger percentage of the players they develop, so that they are actually training players for sale value rather than trying to take them from league 4/tier 4 to the top.  I personally see too much wasted wage budget on middle aged guys that have stopped development because they reached the tier max that teams should have sold.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part6.txt:3139 | [2026-06-20 17:27] furysk : Heavier scouting investment cost - I think that veterans of the world should have a larger 'book' on them, and should have smaller ranges of skills and of potential values than players who are less experienced.  I would make barely experienced a wide range, somewhat slightly less wide ranged, fairly experienced even less range, and highly experienced should come with very little doubt as to what they can do.  Scouts should have plenty of communication with scouts between regions, and this should allow them to find more information on just basic phone conversations and tapes they can get for free.  Slowing down human players on building their teams is more frustrating than a good thing.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part6.txt:9816 | [2025-05-21 14:38] yasen_gg : 1. On FWD - it's a mixed bag. FDW were hard to make sense of. The replacements are somewhat better, but not completely. Creativity and Pressing always have an effect, while Set Pieces are situational.  2. Spider Graph - I agree, the old one was more intuitive. The new one still works, but is combining a lot of skills, so I get it if someone is confused (I am too sometimes).  3. Buildup getting added - Also share this sentiment to an extent, but mostly because I LOVED watching full games. Abstracting away the buildup has been controversial, but I don't think the game is worse off.   4. Transfer windows - I was a big advocate of adding them because I hated how a given percentage of the contract is always wasted due to renewals happening before expiry. However, given how dynamic the game is and how often things change - being able to transfer players in and out is essential. Example - Alin knowing he'll be relegating a couple of weeks in advance has been spending his time to prepare adequately for next season. This would have been impossible if we had the transfer windows. Another example is how you play the game - you've made so many changes at Munster - now imagine joining and waiting a whole month before being allowed to tinker with the squad. I think down the line, having this flexibility of instant impact via the market is key and it's overall a positive.  5. Contracts ending end of the season ties up with the previous point, so won't be repeating myself.  6. I actually agree here - I've even been advocating for more player filled divisions and even adding timespan to the game, e.g. - we all play 3 seasons and then the game starts all over again and this is how we accumulate score. I also said to Derby that the first season of the game where there was not a single bot team in D4 was amazing. Chaotic, but amazing. Then again - there's a charm in being able to fight off the odds and win D1 against bots that were created as unbeatable. No one has done it yet, but it makes the chase even more tempting. So yeah - I think this one has tradeoffs, but I think both approaches are equally interesting/rewarding.  7. On mentors - I also kind of agree here - I don't really like how they work, I want them to exist, but I also have doubts about how they're currently being implemented. That being said - I know why Derby does things this way and I agree it's the right direction. The execution is not perfect, but I'm sure over time we'll find a way to make them feel less unpredictable.  8. On player graphs visible by default - I think this is a byproduct of the game not providing enough data. Seeing the spider graph is too powerful and I agree it should be hidden behind a scout usage, but if we had more overall team data, I think this problem will be solved immediately.  9. Also kind of agree as you don't really have proper training facilities, because everyone maxes out 1 of the 3 just so they can get max possible intensity from one of the areas. Splitting mentors over all 4 is stupid as well as we keep limiting our personal training to "Technical" only. And let's face it - everyone maxes out Technical because Finishing and Handling are the most important skills in the game. So yes - having training unified was more intuitive and allowed more freedom.  Overall, I think you are bringing valid points - there are issues in the game, but the game itself relies on players like you providing feedback. You've been with us for a long time and know how the game works (and how it worked), so it will definitely be a loss if you decide to stop playing (which I hope you won't).
